- `bellcurvebands`: bell-curve bands: a normal-distribution bell curve per tile with the area under it split into four brightness bands by the number of standard deviations from the mean (within 1σ, 1 to 2σ, 2 to 3σ, beyond 3σ) and a tick at each σ; σ is picked by the seed from 0.07, 0.09 and 0.11 UV and the height is fixed. The band areas follow the 68–95–99.7 rule. No axis labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `roundingmodes`: rounding modes: a comparison table per tile: 12 values of x (from −3.5 to 6.5, picked by the seed from a pool of 20, ascending) with five roundings — FL (floor), CE (ceiling), TR (toward zero), RN (halves away from zero) and RE (halves to even); in rows where x is exactly a half the RN and RE columns are darker. A zero result has no minus sign. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `binaryaddition`: binary addition: three column additions per tile, each adding two random 8-bit numbers: small carry digits above the columns that receive them, then A, B with a "+" sign, a rule and the 9-bit sum (darker sum row); every column obeys the full-adder rule. The numbers come from the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `abeliansandpile`: abelian sandpile: the stable sandpile left after 4000, 5200 or 6400 grains (picked by the seed) are dropped on the centre of an 81 × 81 grid (a cell with more than 3 grains gives to its four neighbours), painted in four brightnesses for 0 to 3 grains per cell, with the eightfold symmetry of the square and a 0-grain background outside the pile. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `percolationclusters`: percolation clusters: a 48 × 48 site percolation per tile: every cell is open with probability p (0.56, 0.5927 or 0.63, picked by the seed) and edge-connected open cells form clusters, with the largest darkest, those of at least 8 cells next and smaller ones lighter, and closed cells in the background colour. Clusters do not join across tile boundaries. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `gcdtable`: GCD table: a 12 × 12 table per tile: the header row and column each hold 12 consecutive integers starting at 1, 5, 13 or 25 (picked separately by the seed), and the body prints the greatest common divisor of each cell, lightest for coprime pairs (1) and darker for larger divisors. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bitwiselogic`: bitwise logic: four blocks per tile, each applying AND, OR, XOR or NAND bit by bit to two random 8-bit numbers A and B (each once, in a seed-determined order), with the operation name at the left of the B row and the result row below the rule, whose 1 bits have a darker background. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `logisticbifurcation`: logistic bifurcation: a bifurcation diagram of x → r·x·(1−x) per tile: the horizontal axis is the growth rate r (range picked by the seed from 2.5–4, 3.4–4 and 3.55–3.95) and the vertical axis the long-run value x (0 to 1), with 256 × 256 cells shaded by the number of recorded values in them, showing the fixed point, period doubling, the chaotic band and the period-3 window. Start value 0.4, no axis labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `burningship`: Burning Ship fractal: smooth escape-time contours of the Burning Ship fractal (z → (|Re z| + i|Im z|)² + c) per tile with non-escaping points darkest; the view is picked by the seed from four preset regions and the coordinates are folded so the picture is continuous across tile edges and mirrored. No axis labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `modularpowers`: modular powers: a 10 × 10 table per tile with exponent k = 1 to 10 and base a = 2 to 11, the body printing the remainder of a^k divided by m (m picked by the seed from 13, 15, 17, 21 and 25), cells equal to 1 darker and whole rows of bases sharing a factor with m pale grey. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `primefactors`: prime factors: 12 rows per tile, each with an integer from 2 to 99 on the left (12 distinct values picked by the seed, ascending) and its prime factorization on the right (smallest first, joined by ×, for example 2×2×3×7); rows whose number is prime have a darker background. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `baseconversion`: base conversion: 12 rows per tile, each an integer from 0 to 255 (12 distinct values picked by the seed, ascending) in decimal, 8-digit binary, 3-digit octal and 2-digit hexadecimal (A to F as capital letters); alternating row backgrounds and darker hexadecimal cells containing A to F. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `caesarcipher`: Caesar cipher: three groups per tile: the alphabet A to M, N to Z and a short message, each with its shifted cipher row below (pale grey); the shift is picked by the seed from 3, 5, 7 and 13 and the message from five preset phrases. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `wordsquares`: symmetric word squares: a word square per tile in which row i equals column i, picked by the seed from six presets (the 5 × 5 SATOR square and five 4 × 4 English squares) with the diagonal cells darker. The words were found from a hand-written list and not checked against a dictionary. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `vectorfieldarrows`: vector field arrows: an arrow at each point of a 10 × 10 grid per tile pointing along a vector field, with length and darkness growing with the size of the vector; the field is picked by the seed from four presets: rotation, saddle, source and spiral. No numeric labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `fordcircles`: Ford circles: for every fraction p/q in lowest terms (0 to 1, denominator up to 4, 6 or 8 picked by the seed) a circle of diameter 1/q² tangent to the number line (the bottom edge of the tile) at p/q, with the circles of Farey neighbours tangent to each other; filled by brightness per denominator with a thin outline. No fraction labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `mobiusgrid`: Moebius grid: a checkered square grid per tile after a Moebius transformation w = (az + b)/(cz + d) (picked by the seed from an inversion, an elliptic and a loxodromic preset), with grid lines becoming circles or lines, the checkerboard shading carried along and a line width uniform on screen; coordinates are folded so the picture is continuous across tile edges and mirrored. No pole markings. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `dipolefieldlines`: dipole field lines: equipotentials (potential V = ln(r₂/r₁) every 0.4) and field lines (angle difference every π/10) of a pair of opposite two-dimensional line charges (separation picked by the seed from three presets) per tile, with different backgrounds for positive and negative potential, small round cores at the charges and a line width uniform on screen; coordinates are folded so the picture is continuous across tile edges and mirrored. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `numberwords`: numbers spelled out in English: 12 rows per tile, each with an integer from 0 to 99 on the left (12 distinct values picked by the seed, ascending) and its English name on the right (such as FORTY TWO); alternating row backgrounds and darker number cells below 20. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `sorobanabacus`: soroban abacus: two seven-rod Japanese abacuses per tile (one heaven bead above the beam and four earth beads below it), beads against the beam counting, each rod showing a digit from 0 to 9; the seven-digit number it shows (chosen by the seed) is printed below in dot-matrix digits. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `boxplots`: box plots: three box plots per tile, each group's 15 values drawn as dots on two alternating rows with the box from Q1 to Q3, the median line and whiskers out to the minimum and maximum above them (quartiles by Tukey's hinges, no interpolation); the three groups lean differently. No axis labels. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `stemandleaf`: stem-and-leaf plot: a plot of 29 integers from 10 to 99 per tile (tens as stems, ones as leaves, ascending) with the leaf of the 15th sorted value (the median) on a darker background; values are the mean of two random numbers, so the middle stems have more leaves. No key. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `substitutionwords`: substitution words: rows of words over 0 and 1 stacked per tile, the first row "0" and each later row the previous one with every letter replaced by a rule; 1 is a dark cell and 0 a mid-grey cell, all rows sharing one cell width and left aligned. The seed picks the rule from the Fibonacci word (0→01, 1→0), Thue–Morse (0→01, 1→10) and period doubling (0→01, 1→00). No markings. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `dicesumbars`: dice sum bars: the distribution of the sum of two n-sided dice (n picked by the seed from 4, 6 and 8) per tile, one column per sum (2 to 2n, printed below) with a stack of squares counting the ordered pairs of faces that make that sum (the likeliest column darker). M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `binaryheaptree`: binary heap tree: a complete binary tree of 4 levels and 15 nodes per tile, each node a circle with a two-digit number from 10 to 99 and parents joined to children by lines; the values satisfy the min-heap (or max-heap) property (decided by the seed, built by insert and sift-up) and the root is darker. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bubblesortpasses`: bubble sort passes: an 8 × 8 table per tile whose first row is a random order of 1 to 8 (decided by the seed) and each later row the result of one more bubble-sort pass (7 passes), with the last k cells of row k (already in their final places) on a darker background and the last row the sorted 1 to 8. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `graphcoloring`: graph colouring: a graph per tile (the cube, the Petersen graph or a five-spoke wheel, picked by the seed) whose vertices are filled with brightnesses by colour class so that no edge joins two equal brightnesses, using as many colours as the graph's chromatic number (2, 3 or 4). No vertex numbers. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `runlengthrows`: run-length rows: five groups per tile, each a row of 20 black and white cells (alternating random runs 2 to 6 long) with its run-length code written below in dot-matrix characters (each run's length plus W or B, for example 5W4B6W4B1W). M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `isbn10checks`: ISBN-10 check digits: 11 random ISBN-10 numbers (g-ppp-ttttt-c) per tile, the first 9 digits decided by the seed and the last (on a darker background) the check digit that makes 10·d₁ + 9·d₂ + … + 1·d₁₀ a multiple of 11 (10 is written X); alternating row backgrounds. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `projectilearcs`: projectile arcs: two fountains repeated vertically per tile, with five parabolas at the same speed and launch angles 15° to 75° flying to both sides from the middle line and landing on a ground line, plus a fainter safety parabola touching every path; the maximum range is one of three picked by the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `sternbrocottree`: binary tree of fractions: four levels (1, 2, 4 and 8 fractions) per tile written in dot-matrix characters and linked to their children by straight lines; the seed picks a Stern–Brocot or Calkin–Wilf tree. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cycloidcusps`: cycloid cusps: 3 × 3 cells per tile, each with an epicycloid or hypocycloid with k cusps and a pale grey fixed circle (row 1 epicycloids k = 1, 2, 3; row 2 hypocycloids k = 3, 4, 5; row 3 picked by the seed: epicycloids k = 4, 5, 6 or hypocycloids k = 6, 7, 8); the seed also picks one of four rotations. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.9 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `zeckendorfrows`: Zeckendorf representation rows: two columns of 12 rows, 24 consecutive integers per tile, each row giving the decimal number and its eight-digit Zeckendorf representation (Fibonacci place values 34, 21, 13, 8, 5, 3, 2, 1, never two neighbouring 1s); the seed picks the first number from 1 to 31, and odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `ferrersdiagrams`: Ferrers diagrams: a 5 × 3 grid per tile showing the 15 integer partitions of 7 as rows of boxes (left aligned, each diagram centred) in reverse lexicographic order; the seed picks the English or French style and whether the diagrams are transposed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.7 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `pairingtables`: pairing-function tables: an 8 × 8 table per tile with each cell writing the natural number that pairs its column x and row y, on a faint checkerboard background; the seed picks the Cantor (diagonals), Szudzik (square shells) or Morton (Z-order bit interleaving) rule and whether x and y are swapped. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `hexdumprows`: hex dump rows: 14 rows of random bytes per tile, each a two-digit hex offset, six bytes and the matching text (capital letters, digits and spaces, with about 14% control codes shown as "."); the offset grows by 6 per row from a seed-picked start, and odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `pythagoreantriples`: Pythagorean triples: 12 primitive triples per tile, each row giving Euclid's m and n and a = m² − n², b = 2mn, c = m² + n² (the c column on a darker background); m is at most 9, which gives 18 pairs, and the seed picks 12 and sorts them by hypotenuse. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `figuratenumbers`: figurate-number dot patterns: 4 rows × 5 columns per tile (n = 1 to 5), each row one kind (triangular, square, oblong n × (n+1) and centred square diamond), each figure centred in its cell; the seed shuffles the order of the four kinds from top to bottom, and alternate rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `dyckpaths`: Dyck paths: 14 cells (2 columns × 7 rows) per tile, each showing one of the C₄ = 14 different Dyck paths of semilength 4 (eight steps that never go below the baseline and return to it) as a polyline with vertex dots and a grey baseline; the seed shuffles the order and odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.7 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `sacksspiral`: Sacks spiral: the whole tile places the natural number n at radius c·√n and angle 2π·√n and draws only the primes as dark round dots; the seed picks the last number (900, 1200 or 1600) and whether to draw all primes, only those 4k + 1 or only those 4k + 3. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `sierpinskipascal`: Pascal-parity triangles: the whole tile is a 2^d × 2^d grid (d = 5, 6 or 7 picked by the seed) whose cell in column i and row j is dark when C(i + j, i) is odd (the Sierpiński triangle), plus the same picture turned half a turn; the seed also decides left-right mirroring. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cobwebplots`: cobweb plots: the whole tile draws a cobweb plot of the logistic map f(x) = r·x·(1 − x) with a thin square frame, the grey parabola and diagonal and the dark cobweb (14 iterations); the seed picks one of 5 values of r and 4 of x₀ (20 combinations). M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `divisorcountbars`: divisor-count bars: the whole tile is a bar chart of 64 consecutive integers (the start picked by the seed from 1 to 400), each bar a stack of small boxes with one box per divisor (so its height is d(n)), with primes darkest and perfect squares lightest and a grey baseline underneath. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `timestablecircles`: times-table circles: a 3 × 3 grid per tile, each cell a circle with 72 equally spaced points joined by thin lines from point i to point k·i (modulo 72), the nine multipliers k taken by the seed from 2 to 20 by a shuffle (all different), plus a grey ring, dark dots and a checkerboard background. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.9 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `gaussianprimes`: Gaussian primes: the whole tile is a 40 × 40 window of the Gaussian integers a + bi with a dark dot in each cell of a Gaussian prime (norm prime when neither a nor b is 0; when one is 0, the other of absolute value a prime 4k + 3) on a checkerboard background; the seed picks the window corner from six choices. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `josephuscircle`: Josephus circle: the whole tile shows n people in a circle with every k-th one eliminated, each disc showing the order in which that person leaves, the survivor on a darker grey disc and k written large in the middle; the seed picks n (17, 19, 21, 23 or 24) and k (2 to 5). M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.9 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `periodictablecells`: periodic-table cells: the whole tile draws the layout of the first four periods (elements 1 to 36) in 18 columns and 4 rows, each cell with the atomic number above and a capital-letter symbol below and blanks where the table has no element; the seed decides whether the three background shades mean block, metal / metalloid / nonmetal, or solid / liquid / gas at 25 °C. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `circleoffifths`: circle of fifths: the whole tile is a circle of fifths with the 12 major keys on the outer ring (each a perfect fifth above the previous one), their relative minors in the middle and the number of sharps or flats inside, on 12 alternating sectors with three thin rings; the seed picks the key at the top (12 choices) and whether the fifths run clockwise or anticlockwise. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.9 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `roulettewheel`: roulette wheel: the whole tile is the ring of the 37 pockets of a European wheel in the actual wheel order, each showing its number, red pockets mid grey, black pockets dark and the zero light, with a grey disc in the middle; the seed picks the pocket at the top (37 choices) and the reading direction. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.9 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `roundrobintable`: round-robin schedule: the whole tile is the schedule of n teams (8 or 10 picked by the seed) by the circle method, n − 1 rounds each with its matches on one row, every team playing once per round and every pair meeting exactly once; the team numbering is shuffled by the seed and odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `kaprekarsteps`: Kaprekar steps: the whole tile is split into 2 × 2 blocks, each a chain of Kaprekar's routine from a seed-picked four-digit start, every step subtracting the digits sorted ascending from the same sorted descending (written "large-small=difference") until 6174 (at most 7 steps), with the difference on a darker cell. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `lookandsayrows`: look-and-say rows: the whole tile lists the terms of the look-and-say sequence from top to bottom (each term reads the previous one aloud: 1, 11, 21, 1211, 111221…), the first term picked by the seed from 1 to 6, until the next term would exceed 26 digits; the first digit of each row is on a darker cell and odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `happynumberchains`: happy-number chains: the whole tile writes 12 chains of the sum of the squares of the digits (starts from 2 to 99 picked by the seed, all different, for example 19>82>68>100>1) that end at 1 or stop at the first 4 (every unhappy number falls into the same cycle); rows ending at 1 have a darker cell under their first number and odd rows a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `recamanarcs`: Recaman arcs: the whole tile draws each step of the Recaman sequence as a semicircle between two numbers (above the horizontal line for odd steps, below for even, with a dot for every number); the seed picks 40, 50, 60 or 70 terms. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `thuemorsegrid`: Thue–Morse grid: the whole tile is a square grid whose cell k is dark or light by the parity of the number of 1 bits in k plus an offset, with a lower mid-grey groove between cells; the seed picks the number of cells per side (8, 16, 32), the offset (0 to 255) and row or column numbering. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `digitalroottable`: digital-root table: the whole tile is a square table whose cells hold the digital root (1 to 9) of i × j, i + j or i² + j² (the seed picks one of three; i and j count from 1), toned by root (1 darkest, 9 lightest) with the root written as a 3 × 5 dot-matrix digit, with 9, 12 or 18 cells per side and a lower mid-grey groove between cells. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `henonattractor`: Henon attractor: the whole tile is a density plot of the Henon map (x′ = 1 − a·x² + y, y′ = b·x) iterated 600,000 times; the seed picks one of four chaotic parameter pairs and possible mirroring, y is stretched about 1.5 times relative to x, and a blank margin lets the tile repeat seamlessly. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `golombrulers`: Golomb rulers: the whole tile draws nine optimal Golomb rulers with 3 to 11 marks from top to bottom (no two pairs of marks at the same distance, one scale, the longest 72 units), each with a body, a small tick at every unit and tall mark lines; the seed mirrors each ruler at random. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bitwisetable`: bitwise table: the whole tile is a square table whose cells hold i AND j, i OR j or i XOR j (the seed picks one of three; i and j count from 0), toned linearly by value and written in binary as small squares (a square is a 1), with 8, 16 or 32 cells per side and a lower mid-grey groove between cells. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `peanocurve`: Peano curve: the whole tile draws the Peano space-filling curve through the cell centres, with order 2, 3 or 4 picked by the seed (9, 27 or 81 cells per side), tone shading from the darkest at its start to a lighter blue-grey at its end, and one of eight mirrorings/turns picked at random; the line is about half a cell wide and a blank margin lets the tile repeat seamlessly. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cliffordattractor`: Clifford attractor: the whole tile is a density plot of the Clifford map (x′ = sin(a·y) + c·cos(a·x), y′ = sin(b·x) + d·cos(b·y)) iterated 800,000 times; the seed picks one of five parameter sets and possible mirroring, the window is the exact range of the map and a blank margin lets the tile repeat seamlessly. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `voronoicells`: Voronoi cells: the whole tile is a Voronoi partition with one jittered site per grid cell (5, 7 or 9 per side, jitter picked by the seed, the grid wraps so it is seamless); every point takes the random grey of its nearest site, with thin dark borders and a small dark dot at every site. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `euclidsquares`: Euclid squares: the whole tile draws the Euclidean algorithm as squares (a rectangle is cut along its longer side from the top left corner into the largest squares, then the leftover likewise until nothing is left; six rectangles, one of eight orientations picked by the seed), the squares toned from dark to light in the order of cutting, with thin dark borders. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `youngtableaux`: Young tableaux: the whole tile is 2 × 2 panels, each drawing a standard Young tableau of 10 boxes (a partition-of-10 shape holding 1 to 10 once each, strictly increasing along rows and columns); the seed picks four different shapes, fills them at random and chooses English or French drawing and transposing; boxes are toned from dark to light by number with 3 × 5 dot-matrix digits and thin dark lines between boxes. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `braiddiagram`: braid diagram: the whole tile draws 4, 5 or 6 strands from top to bottom with two neighbouring strands crossing at every level (one over, one under, the under one cut by the dark outline of the over one), never the same pair twice in a row, each strand keeping a grey tone fixed by its starting place so the tone order at the bottom is the permutation of the braid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `countingrods`: counting rods: the whole tile is eight rows, each writing a decimal number of 2 to 5 digits at the left in 3 × 5 dot-matrix digits and again at the right as counting rods (units at the right; 1 to 5 are that many rods, 6 to 9 a bar plus 1 to 4 rods, 0 an empty place; units, hundreds and ten-thousands vertical, tens and thousands horizontal); odd rows have a faint band. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `quadtreecells`: quadtree cells: the whole tile is an adaptive quadtree whose squares are split in four while the shape function differs in sign among a square's corners and middle (the shape's edge passes through), at depth 2 to 6 (finest 64 × 64); the seed picks one of 12 preset shapes (discs, rings, ellipses, wave lines, slanted lines), cells inside the shape are darker and deeper ones darker still, with dark borders that get thinner for smaller cells. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `lsystemplant`: L-system plant: the whole tile draws a branching plant by turtle graphics; the seed picks one of four grammars with one of two iteration counts each and possible mirroring, scaled uniformly and centred, with lines shading with branch depth from the dark trunk to lighter grey-green tips. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `htreefractal`: H-tree: the whole tile draws an H-tree (every segment carries a perpendicular segment 1/√2 as long at each end, to a depth of 6 to 9 picked by the seed, possibly turned a quarter), thinner and lighter with depth. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `wangtiles`: Wang tiles: the whole tile is a grid of square tiles with a grey colour on each of the four sides, split into four triangles by diagonals, with touching sides always the same colour (also across the tile edge); the seed picks 6, 8 or 10 tiles per side, 3 or 4 colours and every edge colour. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `marchingsquares`: marching-squares contours: a wrapping metaball field sampled at grid corners (dark dots above the threshold, lighter below), its contour drawn with the classic rules (saddles included) as straight segments per cell; the seed picks one of ten ball sets and a grid of 16, 24 or 32. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `tsquarefractal`: T-square fractal: in each cell (2 or 3 per side) a half-width square in the middle and half-width squares on every corner, recursively for 4 to 6 levels, deeper ones on top with the tone stepping by level and a thin outline; the seed picks the cell count, the depth and whether deeper levels are lighter or darker. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `superformulacells`: superformula cells: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid, each cell holding one closed Gielis superformula curve filled with a tone and thinly outlined; every cell picks its own one of ten parameter rows, three scales and six tones. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `paleygraphmatrix`: Paley graph adjacency matrix: a p x p grid of cells (p one of 13, 17, 29, 37, 41, 53) where column i, row j shows whether i − j (mod p) is zero (the diagonal), a square (an edge) or a non-square (no edge), with thin lines between cells. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `candlestickcharts`: candlestick charts: three stacked panels of 16, 24 or 32 candles; bodies from open to close, light when rising and dark when falling, with thin wicks to the high and low, each open equal to the previous close and the series wrapping round (a closed walk). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `tetrominoes`: tetromino tiling: an 8 x 8 or 12 x 12 grid covered exactly, with wrap-around, by the seven tetrominoes (I, O, T, S, Z, J, L, turned but not turned over), one grey tone per type and dark lines between pieces; the tiling comes from a seed-shuffled depth-first search. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `errordiffusion`: error-diffusion dithering: a smooth periodic grey picture sampled on a 48, 64 or 96 grid, rounded to light or dark cell by cell with one of six kernels (Floyd–Steinberg, Atkinson, Jarvis–Judice–Ninke, Stucki, Burkes, Sierra) spreading the error to later cells; the seed picks the grid, kernel and picture. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `worleycells`: Worley (cellular) noise: an n x n grid (6, 8, 10) with one jittered feature point per cell, F1/F2 from the 5 x 5 cells around in one of three metrics, coloured by F1, F2, F2 − F1 or F1 / F2, with a dome of height around each point; the seed picks the grid, jitter, metric and function. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `equalizerbars`: equalizer LED bars: 16, 24 or 32 bands, each a column of 16, 20 or 24 LED segments lit from the bottom to the band's level with a separate peak segment above, three grey zones by height, dark unlit segments and gaps; levels are two sines plus scatter and wrap left to right. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `intelhexrecords`: Intel HEX records: 27 lines of ":10AAAA00" + 16 data bytes + checksum (all bytes of a record add up to 0 modulo 256) and the end record ":00000001FF", in 3 x 5 pixel characters, the start address picked by the seed and rising by 16 per line; the six fields each have an ink tone. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `flagpatterns`: flag patterns: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid of 3:2 geometric flags (horizontal/vertical tricolour, bicolour, quartered, Nordic cross, saltire, central disc, seven stripes with a canton) on a mid-tone cloth with a thin dark outline; the seed picks each cell's design and three different tones; they do not stand for real flags. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `gaugedials`: gauge dials: a 2 x 2 or 3 x 3 grid of round analogue gauges with a bezel, a pale face, three tone zones (two borders picked by the seed), 11 long ticks with 4 short ticks between them (from -135 to +135 degrees), a needle with a short tail and a hub; the needle position of each gauge is picked by the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `qrcodemodules`: QR-style modules: a square symbol of 21, 25 or 29 modules (versions 1 to 3) with a 4-module quiet zone, the finder patterns, separators, timing patterns, dark module and alignment pattern (versions 2 and 3) at the standard places, every other module (format areas included) random, not a readable QR code. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `mahjongdots`: mahjong dot tiles: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid of 3:4 rounded tiles on a cloth, each with 1 to 9 dots in the usual circles-suit arrangements; a dot is a ring, a gap and a centre, and tiles have a darker rim; the seed picks counts and tones. The arrangements are typed from memory, not checked against a real set. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `smithchart`: Smith chart: circles of constant resistance and arcs of constant reactance (thin lines) and the axis inside the unit circle, plus a darker standing-wave-ratio circle with a dot on it joined to the middle by a line; the seed picks the quarter turns, the circle radius and the dot position; cloth outside. No scale text, not for engineering use. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `compassrose`: compass rose: a 16-point star (4 long, 4 medium, 8 short thin triangles, the clockwise half dark and the other half a mid tone) with a small ring at its centre, inside a dial with a tick every 5 degrees (longer every 45, longest every 90); the seed turns the star by a multiple of 5 degrees. No text, not for navigation. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `windbarbs`: wind barbs: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid of weather map wind symbols, a station ring with a staff pointing the way the wind blows from and, from its tip inwards, a flag per 50 knots, a long barb per 10 and a short barb for 5 (a lone short barb set one step in), calm as a double ring; speeds (0 to 65) and directions (multiples of 10 degrees) picked by the seed. Wind symbols only, not for operational weather use. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `rankchevrons`: rank chevrons: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid of round cloth patches, each sewn with 1 to 4 chevrons (V shapes), 0 to 2 curved bars and an optional pip inside a thin ring; the counts are picked by the seed. Plain shapes, not any real rank. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief.
- `segmenttree`: segment tree: a pyramid of boxes (root on top, 3, 4 or 5 levels below it), leaves of random values picked by the seed and every box above the mean of its two children (the tone is the value); the seed picks a range of leaves and the fewest boxes that cover exactly it are outlined. The mean instead of the sum keeps tones between 0 and 1. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `vicsekfractal`: Vicsek fractal: 27 or 81 cells a side, a cell is in the fractal when every one of its base 3 digit pairs fits the plus or the cross pattern; cells in it are toned darker the more of their digit pairs are (middle, middle), with a gap between cells. The depth and the pattern are picked by the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `jacobisymbols`: Jacobi symbols: 24 or 32 cells a side, the cell in row r and column c showing the Jacobi symbol (a / b) (b odd): +1 light, -1 dark, 0 (shared factor) mid tone; the seed picks the size and the window position (within a 4N by 4N block). The sampler uses quadratic reciprocity, the test the prime factorisation and Euler's criterion. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `fenwicktree`: Fenwick tree: N (16 or 32) positions and a row per level, the bar of index i covering the lowbit(i) positions ending at i; the seed picks a query index and an update index, the bars on the prefix-query path get a dark outline and those on the update path a light outline inside it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `semaphorefigures`: semaphore figures: a 3 x 3 or 4 x 4 grid of stick figures each holding a flag on each arm at one of 8 directions (45 degree steps, the two arms differing), the flags split along their diagonal into two tones; the seed picks the grid and the directions, no letters are encoded. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `hofstadterq`: Hofstadter Q sequence: a scatter plot of 128 consecutive terms (one dot per column, the height scaled so the smallest and biggest term sit at the margins), the seed picking where the window starts (term 1, 129, 257, 513, 1025 or 2049), with the Q = n / 2 line and horizontal rulings in 8. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `countertimings`: counter timing diagram: a clock row (16 periods) and the four outputs of a 4-bit counter changing together on every rising edge; the seed picks how it counts (binary up or down, Gray code, Johnson twisted ring, one-hot ring) and the starting state, with faint vertical guides at the periods. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `qamconstellation`: QAM constellation: a 2 x 2, 4 x 4 or 8 x 8 grid of symbols, each cell with its constellation point and the bit squares of the in-phase and quadrature axes (filled 1, empty frame 0); the seed picks the size and Gray or plain binary coding per axis (identical at 2 x 2), with slightly different paper tones per quadrant and two axes. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `chorddiagrams`: guitar chord diagrams: a 2 x 2 or 3 x 3 grid of chord boxes (6 strings, a nut, 5 frets), a filled dot a fingered string, a ring an open one and a cross a muted one; ten first-position chords (written from memory as fret numbers per string and checked by the tests through the notes of the strings in standard tuning), the seed picking the grid and which chords are shown. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `waterfallchart`: waterfall chart: a start bar from zero, floating bars for steps up and down (whole numbers of 3 to 18, the running total kept between 10 and 90) and a total bar from zero, with thin connectors at the running level, grid lines and a base line; the seed picks the number of steps (6, 10 or 14) and the values. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `intervalpartition`: interval partitioning: up to 28 intervals packed by the greedy rule (in order of start, into the first free lane) into 6 lanes, with a depth strip of one column per time unit under them; the lanes used equal the tallest depth. The seed picks the starts and the lengths. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `ecdfplot`: empirical cumulative distribution plot: a step curve for n (8, 12, 16 or 20) data points rising by 1 / n at each, with a closed dot at the new level and an open ring at the old one, a rug under it and faint quartile guides; the seed picks n and the moves of the points. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `spanningtree`: minimum spanning tree: 8, 12 or 16 points on a 24 x 24 lattice joined by the edges the Kruskal algorithm accepts, toned from dark to light by the order of acceptance; the seed picks the number of points and where they are. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `kdtreesplits`: k-d tree: 15 or 31 points (one in every row and column) split at the median by alternating vertical and horizontal lines that run to the edges of their region, lighter and thinner the deeper they are; the seed picks the number of points, where they are and the direction of the first split. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bsttreeorder`: binary search tree: nodes at their order along the width and at their depth down the height, 11, 15 or 19 keys inserted in a seeded order shown by the node tone from dark to light, edges to the parents; a shuffle that would go deeper than level 6 is shuffled again. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `karnaughmap`: Karnaugh map: a Boolean function of 3 or 4 variables as a 2 x 4 or 4 x 4 grid, filled where the function is 1, with Gray-coded row and column labels and the same function as a truth-table column beside it; the seed picks the number of variables and the function. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `nearesttour`: nearest-neighbour tour: 8, 10 or 12 points on a 24 x 24 lattice (no three on a line), visited from a ringed start by always going on to the nearest unvisited point, each leg toned by when it is travelled and the path left open; the seed picks the number of points, where they are and the start. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `editdistance`: edit-distance table: two short strings of 4 to 7 symbols (three symbols, drawn as one to three dots) and their Levenshtein distance table (0 lightest, 7 darkest), with a frame on the cells of the cheapest way back (diagonal first, then up, then left); the seed picks the two strings. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `hashchains`: hash table with chaining: 6, 7 or 8 buckets and 10, 14 or 18 keys of 7 bits inserted in turn, each into the bucket of its remainder, every chain a row of boxes with the key in binary and a frame toned by insertion order, no chain longer than 5; the seed picks the numbers of buckets and keys and the keys. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `huffmancodes`: Huffman code: 5, 6 or 7 symbols, one to a row with a bar as long as its frequency and its code (a dark square for 1, an empty frame for 0), the most frequent first; the seed picks the number of symbols and the frequencies. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bodeplots`: Bode plot: the gain (in decibels) and the phase (in degrees) of a first-order low-pass or a second-order low-pass, high-pass or band-pass filter against a log frequency axis from 1 to 1000, a grid line at each decade; the seed picks the type, the corner and the quality. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `sankeyflows`: Sankey diagram: three columns of nodes (3, 3 or 4, and 3) joined by S-shaped bands, each left node sending 1 to 3 units to each middle node and each middle node passing what it received on to the right in three parts, every node as tall as the flow through it; the seed picks the number of middle nodes and all the flows. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `histogrambins`: histogram: 20, 28 or 36 values between 0 and 1 (a row of ticks at the bottom, leaning to the middle) and bars for 8, 10 or 12 bins as tall as the number of values in each, with a mark below the baseline at each bin edge; the seed picks the numbers and the values. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `zordercurve`: Z-order (Morton) curve: the cells of a 4 x 4 or 8 x 8 grid joined by segments in the order of their number (even bits the column, odd bits the row), toned from dark to light, with a dot at each cell centre; the seed picks the size and one of eight turns and flips. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `lorenzcurve`: Lorenz curve: 8, 10 or 12 incomes as bars in rank order below, and above a square plot of cumulative share of the total against cumulative share of the people, with dots and polyline, the diagonal line of equality and a lighter fill between them whose area is half the Gini coefficient; the seed picks the number of incomes and the incomes. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `ganttschedule`: Gantt chart: 6, 7 or 8 tasks (1 to 4 units long) on a 16-unit time axis, each depending on none to two of the tasks above it and starting as soon as the last of them has finished, with the dependencies drawn as links with dots; the seed picks the number of tasks, the durations and the dependencies. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `quicksortsteps`: quicksort steps: 8, 9 or 10 shuffled bars, a row for the start and one for each partition (Lomuto, about the last bar of the range), with a bracket over the range partitioned and a dot over the place the pivot ended in, and the bars in their place darker; the seed picks the number of bars and the shuffle. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `binarysearchsteps`: binary search steps: 12, 14 or 16 rising bars with a tick at the left for the target height, one row per step: the bars still open are darker, the middle one darkest with a dot, and the next row keeps only the half that can still hold the target, until it is found or no bar is left; the seed picks the number of bars, their heights and the target. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `dijkstragrid`: Dijkstra shortest-path grid: a 7, 8 or 9 cell square of cells costing 1 to 4 or (three in ten, the darkest) 20, with a dark line for the cheapest way from the top left (small dot) to the bottom right (large dot), moving to a side or up and down; the seed picks the size and the costs. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `kmpfailure`: Knuth-Morris-Pratt failure table: three a/b patterns of 8 to 10 letters (a repeated start of 2 to 4 letters with one letter changed), with a bar under each letter for the length of the longest border up to it; the seed picks the lengths, the letters and the changed ones. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `vernierscale`: vernier scale readings: five readings of a 28-unit main scale and a plate of 11 ticks spaced nine tenths of a unit apart, with exactly one plate tick lined up with the main scale and drawn longer and darker, its number being the tenths; the seed picks the whole units (1 to 17) and the tenths. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `paretochart`: Pareto chart: 7, 8 or 9 bars sorted from the tallest (heights 1 to 30, mostly small), with a line of dots for the running total as a share of the whole (the last at 100%) and a thin line across at 80%; the seed picks the number of bars and their heights. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `radixsortpasses`: radix sort passes: 10, 12 or 14 numbers from 0 to 63, three passes each on one base 4 digit: the bars of a row are shaded by the digit, the row under it is their stable sort by it, and the last row is sorted and drawn in the darkest tone; the seed picks the size and the numbers. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `mergesortruns`: merge sort runs: 13 to 16 shuffled bars, each row merging neighbouring pairs of runs of the row above into runs twice as long and in order (a run without a partner is carried down as it is), neighbouring runs shaded alternately and the last row one run in the darkest tone; the seed picks the number of bars and the shuffle. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bresenhamlines`: Bresenham lines: four 16 by 16 cell panels, each with a line drawn between two end cells by Bresenham's algorithm (filled cells dark, the end cells darker) and the true straight line between their middles drawn thin over it; half way cases go to the cell further along, starting from the left end cell; the seed picks the end cells (at least 8 apart). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bfswavefront`: BFS wavefront: a 10, 11 or 12 cell square with walls (darkest) and one start cell, every open cell shaded by its steps from the start in eight tones (7 or more share one), with the open cells the walls shut off in a ninth, paler tone; the seed picks the size, the walls and the start. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `knapsacktable`: knapsack table: two 0/1 knapsack problems, each with items as rows on the left (weight as 1 to 4 small squares, value as a bar of 1 to 6) and the dynamic programming table on the right (capacities 0 to 9), the bar in a cell being the best value, the cell darker where its item is taken, and the answer cell framed; the seed picks the number of items, the weights and the values. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `prefixtrie`: prefix trie: 6 to 8 words of 2 to 4 letters a, b and c drawn as a tree with the root on the left and a letter per step (the shade of the last stretch of the line), words that begin the same sharing a path, a ring round the dot where a word ends, leaves evenly spaced in the order of the letters and the other nodes halfway between their children; the seed picks the words. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `lrucachetrace`: LRU cache trace: 3 or 4 slots over 14 requests for pages 0 to 5, a column per request: the request on top (shaded by its page), the slots as they are after it (empty ones pale, the slot used framed), and under them a ring for a hit or a dot for a miss; the seed picks the number of slots and the requests. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `funnelchart`: funnel chart: 5 or 6 stages from top to bottom, a centred bar as wide as the number still there (the first at full width, every later one keeping 45 to 95 in a hundred of the one before), slanted sides joining the stages, and at the right of each stage a short bar as long as the share kept over a pale full-length track; the seed picks the number of stages and the shares. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `wordlefeedback`: word guessing feedback: a secret word of 5 letters (6 kinds, shown as the shade of a small square, repeats allowed) and 4 to 6 guesses under it, every tile filled for its feedback (darkest right place, middle elsewhere and not used up, palest none), the secret drawn at the bottom; the seed picks the secret and the guesses. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `nimgame`: Nim game: 3 or 4 heaps (a row each) as bars as tall as the number of stones (1 to 7 at the start), a column per state; the first player leaves an exclusive or (nim-sum) of 0 when it can and otherwise moves at random like the second, and from the fifth state on a random move takes the whole heap; the heap just taken from is in the move tone with the stones taken as a pale ghost, and three bits under them show the nim-sum as filled dots (1) and rings (0); the seed picks the number of heaps, the stones and the random moves. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `gamblersruin`: gambler's ruin: 5 lanes of one gambler each, starting with 1 to 7 coins and winning or losing one a round (chance 0.4/0.5/0.6, shown by one, two or three small squares at the left of the lane) until 0, 8 or 24 rounds; a dot per round with lines for 0 and 8 coins; the seed picks the starts, chances and results. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `bloomfilter`: Bloom filter: a row of 24 bits, 6 keys put in (each shown as 3 dots at the bits its hashes point to) and 6 questions (2 for keys that went in, 4 not; at the right end a ring for all bits set and a filled square for a bit not set), where a key that never went in may get a ring too (a false positive); the seed picks the hashes and the questions. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `linearprobing`: linear probing: a hash table of 16 slots in a row with 8, 10 or 12 keys put in one after another, a row after each; a key's home slot is the dot above its row and it goes to the first empty slot from there, round past the last slot to the first; empty slots are pale and the n-th key put in has the n-th darkest shade; the seed picks the number of keys and the home slots. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `unionfindmerges`: union-find merges: 14 elements in a row, the first row the start and each of the next 10 the state after one merge request; the shade of a square is its set (named by the smallest element, set 0 darkest) and the two elements a request names have a dot above; a request inside one set changes nothing; the seed picks the requests. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `fareysequence`: Farey sequences: row n (up to 7, 8 or 9) has a tick between 0 and 1 at every fraction in lowest terms with a denominator up to n (those new in the row darker), a dot under each row marks the same chosen fraction (denominator 11 to 23), and a pale band in the row runs between that fraction's neighbours in the row; the seed picks the number of rows and the chosen fraction. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `lfsrstates`: LFSR states: an 8-bit linear feedback shift register with a row per state (16, 20 or 24, the start not zero); a dark square is 1 and a pale one 0 (bit 7 at the left), with small squares at the right of the row for the bit shifted out and the feedback bit (the exclusive or of the tapped bits, entering at the left); the taps (always including bit 0) are the dots above the first row, one of three sets; the seed picks the start, the number of rows and the taps. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `crcdivision`: CRC division: a 12-bit message followed by 4 zero bits divided by one of three 5-bit polynomials (exclusive or, no carrying); the divisor is the top row, the first row the dividend and each row after it a step (the divisor subtracted when the next bit is set, with a filled dot at the left, else nothing and a ring), the last four bits of the last row (framed) the remainder; the seed picks the message and the divisor. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `kolakoskisequence`: Kolakoski sequence: a sequence that describes itself: runs alternating between two symbols (1 and 2, 2 and 1, 1 and 3 or 3 and 1), the n-th run as long as the n-th symbol; 4 rows hold the first 64 symbols (the shade of a square is its symbol) and 3 rows under them the lengths of the whole runs (the sequence's own beginning); the seed picks the pair of symbols. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `slopegraph`: slope graph: 6, 8 or 10 items, each a dot on a left and on a right axis (one of 20 evenly spaced levels) joined by a straight line; rising lines dark, falling lines mid-toned, level lines pale; the seed picks the number of items, the levels and the pairing. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `astroidladder`: sliding ladder of an astroid: a ladder of fixed length leaning in a corner with its top and foot on the two axes, drawn at 14, 18 or 22 places (mirrored into all four corners), each with two end dots and a smaller dot where it touches the astroid; the places are even or bunched by a power; the seed picks the number of ladders, the length and the spread. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `eulerlinetriangle`: Euler line of a triangle: an acute triangle's corner dots, circumcircle, three altitudes (meeting at the orthocentre H, a small square), circumcentre O (a small ring), centroid G (a small dot) and the Euler line through them (G a third of the way from O to H); never near equilateral or isosceles; the seed picks the circle's radius, how the arcs differ and the turn. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `arbelos`: Archimedes' arbelos: the big half circle on A to B with two smaller ones inside on A to C and C to B, the line up from C to D on the big arc, and the twin circles on either side of the line, both of radius a b / (a + b); dots at A, B, C and D; the seed picks where C is (share 0.25 to 0.42, either way round). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `contributiongraph`: contribution calendar: five years one under the other, a cell a day in one of five shades by how much was done (a column a week, Sunday at the top), a gap in front of each week that starts a month, and a key of the five shades; the first year is one of twelve listed ones and the rest follow it (leap years included), with the real calendar's days and weekday of January 1; weekends are quieter and some weeks busier. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `pythagoreanproof`: the figure of the theorem of Pythagoras: a right triangle with a square ruled into unit cells on each side (a x a + b x b = c x c cells to count) and a dot at each corner; the sides are one of 3,4,5 / 6,8,10 / 5,12,13 / 8,15,17, and the order of the short sides, the turn and the mirror come from the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `keplerorbit`: Kepler's second law: an elliptical orbit with the sun at a focus, positions marked at equal times (equal mean-anomaly steps), the lines from the sun cutting it into equal-area wedges shaded in two alternating tones; the seed picks the semi-major axis, eccentricity, number of wedges, direction and time of the first position. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `eisensteinprimes`: Eisenstein primes: the triangular lattice of a + b w (w a cube root of one), 28 x 28 points, the primes as big dark dots, the other numbers as small light dots and the origin as a medium dot with a ring; the seed picks the origin's place (one of eight); the primes lie the same under a sixth of a turn. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `catalantriangulations`: every way to cut a convex polygon into triangles with diagonals that do not cross (a pentagon in 5, a hexagon in 14, a heptagon in 42 ways, the Catalan numbers), one in each cell of a grid; the seed picks the polygon, the order and the empty cells. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `binarynecklaces`: all the binary necklaces of n beads (dark or light; the same up to turning: 14 of 6 beads, 20 of 7, 36 of 8), each on a ring in one cell of a grid and turned by a different number of beads from cell to cell; the seed picks the number of beads, the order and the empty cell. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `baumsweetgrid`: the Baum-Sweet sequence in the rows of a 64 by 64 grid (from an offset the seed picks), each cell shaded in five steps of tone and height by how many runs of zeros of odd length the binary form of its number has (none, that is b(n) = 1, is the darkest). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `rudinshapirogrid`: the Rudin-Shapiro sequence in the rows of a 64 by 64 grid (from an offset the seed picks), each cell shaded in six steps of tone and height by how many times 11 occurs (overlapping) in the binary form of its number, even (r = 1) light and odd (r = -1) dark; past 5 only the parity goes on. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `paperfoldingruler`: the ruler sequence (how many times 2 divides n) as the six-step tone and height of the cells of a 61 by 61 grid (from an offset the seed picks), with a smaller square in the cells where the regular paper-folding sequence is 1. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `walshmatrix`: a Hadamard matrix of order 8, 16 or 32 (the sign is (-1) to the number of 1s in the binary i AND j, +1 dark and -1 light) with the rows in the natural order or the Walsh order (by the number of sign changes along the row), the signs optionally all turned; the order, the row order and the turn are picked by the seed. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `beattypartition`: the Beatty sequences floor(k a) and floor(k b) (1 / a + 1 / b = 1), which share out the positive whole numbers, laid out in the rows of a 47 by 47 grid (from an offset the seed picks), the first in dark shades and the second in light, each in three steps by the place k modulo 3; the seed picks a among the golden ratio, the square root of 2, the square root of 3 and e. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cycliccellular`: a cyclic cellular automaton on a 20 by 20 torus (each cell has a state from 0 to states - 1 and goes on to the next when at least threshold of its neighbours already have it), run burnIn steps from random states, with four panels showing four steps one after the other, the states from dark to light; the seed picks one of three rules. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `wireworldcircuit`: the WireWorld cellular automaton (empty, conductor, electron head and tail; a conductor becomes a head when exactly one or two of its neighbours are heads) on a 22 by 22 field, the seed picking one of four circuits and a starting step, with four panels showing four steps one after the other. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `duccirows`: the Ducci iteration on a ring of whole numbers (the first row random from 0 to 99 from the seed), each row the absolute differences round the ring of the one above, n rows of n cells (n from 23, 29, 31, 37), a bigger number darker. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `scrabbleboard`: the Scrabble board of 15 by 15 squares (plain, double and triple letter, double and triple word, each with a tone and height) with a game in progress: the crossing words of one of three layouts as letter tiles whose tone goes with the points of the letter in seven steps; the seed picks the layout and one of the eight ways to turn or flip. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `monopolyboard`: the Monopoly board, a ring of 40 spaces round an 11 by 11 square (GO, jail, free parking, go to jail, railroads, utilities, chance, community chest and taxes each with a tone, the streets with a band of one of the eight colour groups along the side towards the middle) round a middle square; the seed picks one of the eight ways to turn or flip. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cribbageboard`: a wooden cribbage board of three lanes, each of two rows of 60 holes in groups of five, each lane with a front and a back peg of its own colour, the front one in a hole the seed picks and the back one 1 to 24 holes behind it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `xiangqiboard`: the xiangqi board with its lines crossing at 90 points holding 32 piece discs (black dark and red light, the tone going with the kind), the river breaking the inner lines down and the diagonals of the two palaces; the seed picks one of six openings and which side is up. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `chessopening`: a framed 8 by 8 chess board (the bottom left corner dark) with the piece discs (white light and black dark, the tone going with the kind) after the first moves of one of eight openings (the first position among them); the seed picks the opening and whether white is at the bottom or the top. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `ludoboard`: a Ludo board of 15 by 15 cells: the cross of four arms and the centre, a yard with four spots (some with a dark token) in each corner, the home lane, start cell and centre triangle of each colour, thin lines between the cells; the seed sets how the four colours go round (eight ways) and the tokens. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `shogiboard`: a 9 by 9 shogi board with ten lines each way and four star points, and the pentagon pieces (the tone going with the kind, the sides told apart by the direction) after the first moves of one of eight openings; the seed picks the opening and whether the first side is at the bottom or the top (the picture turned half way round). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `carromboard`: a carrom board: a wooden frame, a square surface, four pockets, a centre circle of two rings, a base line on each side, the black and white coins on a hexagonal packing (the queen in the middle) and a striker; the seed sets whether the coins are turned by a twelfth of a turn, whether black and white are swapped, which coins are there and where the striker is. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `ninemensmorris`: a Nine Men's Morris board: a wooden frame, three nested squares with four joining lines and 24 points, and the black and white men of the placing phase (the seed picks how many, from 0 to 18, and where; the colours alternate from the side that goes first). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `arnoldcatmap`: Arnold's cat map: a small four-level picture (the letter F, rings or four quarters) after the map (x, y) to (2x+y, x+y) a number of times, as cells; the seed picks the number of cells (32 to 64), the picture and the times (none to one short of the period). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `tablutboard`: a 9 by 9 Tablut board in a wooden frame with lines, the throne, corner marks and the camps, and the disc pieces (attackers dark, defenders light, the king larger) after the first moves of one of eight openings; the seed picks the opening and how many quarter turns the board is turned. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `poolrack`: a pool table: the cloth, the rail, six pockets, eighteen diamonds and the head string, with the fifteen balls racked in a triangle (the 8 in the middle, a solid and a stripe at the back corners, stripes with a white band) and the cue ball; the seed sets which end the rack is at, the order of the balls and the cue ball. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `playingcardpips`: playing cards on a green table, a row for each suit (the order shuffled by the seed) of five consecutive ranks (ace to ten, from a first rank the seed picks), with the pips in the places the usual cards have them (upside down below the middle) and the suit shapes. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `tenniscourt`: a tennis court seen from above, with the baselines, the sidelines of doubles and singles, the service lines, the centre service line, the centre marks, the net and its posts in the dimensions of the rules (feet), the run-off and the ground; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the court is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `badmintoncourt`: a badminton court seen from above, with the baselines, the sidelines of doubles and singles, the short service lines, the long service lines of doubles, the centre lines, the net and its posts on the doubles sidelines in the dimensions of the rules (metres), the run-off and the ground; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the court is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `athleticstrack`: the plan of a 400 metre athletics track seen from above: two straights joined by two half circles, eight lanes with lane lines, the kerb line, the finish line, the infield, the margin and the ground; the lines are drawn wider than the rules so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the track is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `soccerpitch`: a football pitch seen from above, with the touchlines and goal lines, the halfway line, the centre circle and mark, the penalty areas, the goal areas, the penalty marks and arcs and the corner arcs in the dimensions of the laws (metres), the margin and the ground; the lines are drawn wider than the laws so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the pitch is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `hockeyrink`: an ice hockey rink seen from above, with the rounded rink and boards, the centre line, the blue lines, the goal lines, the centre circle and face-off circles, the face-off spots, the goal creases in the dimensions of the NHL rink (feet), and the ground; the lines are drawn wider than the rules so that they show in a tile and red and blue are just two tones; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the rink is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `basketballcourt`: a basketball court seen from above, with the sidelines and baselines, the halfway line, the centre circles, the lanes, the free-throw circles, the backboards and rims, the restricted area arcs and the three-point lines in the dimensions of the NBA court (feet), the margin and the ground; the lines are drawn wider than the rules so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the court is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `handballcourt`: a handball court seen from above, with the sidelines and goal lines, the centre line, the throw-off circle, the goal area lines, the dashed free-throw lines and the 7 and 4 metre lines in the dimensions of the IHF rules (metres), the clear zone and the ground; the lines are drawn four times wider than the rules so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the court is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `highwaylanes`: a four lane road seen from above, with the double yellow centre line, the broken white lane lines between lanes of the same direction (10 feet of line, 30 of gap), the solid white edge lines, the shoulders and the roadside after the MUTCD pavement marking rules (feet); the tile is 120 feet, exactly three cycles of the broken line, so it repeats seamlessly; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the road is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `cricketground`: a cricket ground seen from above, with the pitch, the bowling, popping and return creases, the wickets, the 30 yard fielding circle, the boundary rope and the ground in the dimensions of the MCC laws and the ICC playing conditions (metres); the lines are drawn five times wider than the rules so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the pitch is laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `curlingrink`: ten curling sheets side by side seen from above (a tile of 47.5 metres), with the tee lines, the centre line, the back lines, the hog lines, the hack lines, the side lines, the houses (three circles and the button) at both ends and the back boards in the dimensions of the World Curling rules (metres); thin lines are drawn wider than the rules so that they show in a tile; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and the way the sheets are laid. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `baseballdiamond`: a baseball field seen from above, with the foul lines, the 90 foot diamond and the bases, home plate, the batter's and catcher's boxes, the pitcher's plate and the mound, the infield dirt of radius 95 feet and the infield grass, and the outfield grass in the dimensions of the official baseball rules (feet); the chalk lines are drawn about twice as wide as the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and one of four turns. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `volleyballcourt`: an indoor volleyball court seen from above, with the 18 by 9 court, the centre line, the two attack lines, the five short lines beyond the sidelines, the service marks behind the end lines, the free zone and the floor beyond it in the dimensions of the FIVB rules (metres); the lines are drawn three times and the short lines two times as large as the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the court along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `pickleballcourt`: a pickleball court seen from above, with the 20 by 44 court, the kitchen 7 feet from the net and its line, the centre line, the 30 by 60 playing area round it and the ground beyond in the dimensions of the USA Pickleball rules (feet); the lines are drawn three times as wide as the rules; the kitchen has a tone of its own; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the court along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `squashcourt`: a squash court (singles) seen from above, with the 9.75 by 6.4 court, the short line, the half-court line and the two service boxes, the walls round it and the ground beyond in the dimensions of the WSF court specifications (metres); the lines are drawn two times as wide as the rules and the walls are higher than the floor; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the court along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `gridironfield`: an American football field seen from above, in feet (from memory, not checked): 100 yards between the goal lines and 10 yard end zones, 160 wide, a yard line every 5 yards, two rows of hash marks every yard, mowing stripes and end zone tones, the margin and the ground; the lines and hash marks are drawn larger than the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the field along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `netballcourt`: a netball court seen from above, with the 30.5 by 15.25 court, the lines that divide it into thirds, the 0.9 centre circle, the goal circles of radius 4.9, the 3.05 clear surround and the ground beyond in the dimensions of the World Netball rules (metres); the lines are drawn three times as wide as the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the court along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `snookertable`: a snooker table seen from above, with the 3.569 by 1.778 playing area, the baulk line, the D, the six spots, the cushions, the rail, the six pockets and the floor in the dimensions of the WPBSA rules (metres); the line and the spots are drawn larger than on a table; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little), the baulk end and lays the table along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `archerytarget`: a World Archery 122 centimetre target face seen from the front, with the ten scoring zones in five colours (as different lights and darks), the inner ten, the dividing lines drawn inside the zone of the higher score (none between the light blue and the black or between the black and the white), the centre cross, the boss round it and the ground in the dimensions of the rules (metres); the lines are drawn four times as wide as the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little). M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `runwaymarkings`: the first 780 metres of a precision approach runway seen from above, with the 12 threshold stripes, the centre line, the aiming point marking, the touchdown zone markings (the pair within 50 metres of the aiming point marking left out), the side stripes, the shoulders and the ground in the dimensions of ICAO Annex 14 (metres); the centre line and the side stripes are drawn two times as wide as the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little), which end is the threshold end and lays the runway along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `fieldhockeypitch`: a field hockey pitch seen from above, with the 91.40 by 55.00 pitch, the centre line, the two 23 metre lines, the striking circles, the broken lines 5 metres outside them, the penalty spots, the run-offs (3 and 2, then 2 and 1 metres) and the ground in the dimensions of the FIH (metres); the lines, the dashes and the spots are drawn larger than the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the pitch along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `futsalcourt`: a futsal court seen from above, with the 40 by 20 court, the halfway line and centre circle, the penalty areas, the penalty and second penalty marks with the marks beside them, the corner arcs, the marks outside the court and the lines of the substitution zones, the surround and the ground in the dimensions of law 1 of the FIFA Futsal Laws of the Game (metres; the rules were read); the lines and the small things are drawn four times as large as the rules; the penalty areas have a tone of their own; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little), the touchline with the substitution zones and lays the court along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `tabletennistable`: a table tennis table with its net seen from above, with the 2.74 by 1.525 playing surface, the side and end lines, the doubles centre line, the net across the middle with its ends beyond the side lines and the floor round the table, in the dimensions of the Laws of Table Tennis (ITTF Statutes 2026, sections 2.1 and 2.2, metres; the rules were read); the lines are drawn four times as wide as in the rules; the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the table along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `fencingpiste`: a fencing piste seen from above, with the 14 by 1.5 combat area, the broken centre line, the on-guard lines 2 either side, the rear limit lines at 7, the last 2 metres in a tone of their own, the run-back (its length my choice) and the floor round it, in the dimensions of the FIE Technical Rules (August 2026, t.17 to t.19, metres; the rules were read); the seed picks one of four surfaces (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the piste along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief.
- `code128barcodes`: rows of Code 128 barcodes that decode (start symbol, six symbols, a check symbol calculated again, the stop pattern and the quiet zones, code B or code C by the seed; the table of widths is that of the Wikipedia article, parsed and checked); the seed picks one of four label stocks (the tones shifted together a little) and lays the barcodes along the picture or across it. M0, mortar 0; defaults 1 m nominal tile, 0.0012 UV-relative relief.
